{"candidates": ["F43.0"], "note": "Previously the candidate was F43.0 (Acute stress reaction), and it remains the primary candidate. The patient describes flashbacks, hypervigilance, nightmares, and marked autonomic arousal immediately following a traumatic event (the car crash). However, symptoms have only been present for one week. F43.0 is appropriate when symptoms develop within minutes to hours of an exceptional stressor and resolve within 2–3 days, but in this case, symptoms are persisting beyond that typical timeframe. Despite the ongoing nature, the duration is still less than one month, which rules out F43.1 (Post-traumatic stress disorder), which requires symptoms lasting more than one month. Therefore, F43.0 remains the most appropriate candidate at this time due to the acute onset after trauma and significant symptom burden, even though duration criteria for PTSD are not yet met.", "is_final": false}

At night, I just lie there replaying the crash, and if I do fall asleep, I jerk awake at the slightest sound. Even at work, any sudden noise makes me jump out of my skin and sends my heart racing for minutes.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms every day since the accident, and have they lasted for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have been happening constantly every single day and night since the crash. I haven't had any real break from it, and it feels like it's hanging over me almost all day long.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been ongoing every day since the accident, and do they continue to occur most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. It has been happening every single day without fail since the crash last week. I haven't had a single day of relief, and it really does weigh on me all day long.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms every day since the accident, and do they continue to occur most of the day, every day?"
  }

Yes, that's correct. It has been happening every single day since the accident last week, and I haven't had any relief from it throughout the day.

Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
